Issue-Commit: 0a604b062e573c61c826ce2db7f8eca0b46537c9

# maps.geo synthesizes published basemap assets at request time instead of serving them

> _Note: this issue was identified against the code base at commit `0a604b062e573c61c826ce2db7f8eca0b46537c9`. If you are an issue fixer working on this, the code base you are currently working in can have drifted, so you might need to trace the issue back to the current code base._

## Operation
GetGlyphs

## Description
SHARED FAULT: every asset-serving operation of the Geo Maps service (GetGlyphs, GetSprites, GetStyleDescriptor) returns a STATIC PUBLISHED artifact that AWS keys only on the operation's URI labels/query options - the same bytes in every region, account and run. The emulator does not store those artifacts; it GENERATES a stand-in at request time (a synthesized glyph PBF, a synthesized sprite raster, a computed uniform-grid sprite index, a style document composed from a palette record plus the request knobs). Every one of the 6 causes below is one manifestation of that single fault, and each must be closed before this issue is done. SIX CAUSES ARE BUNDLED HERE.

=== Cause 1 of 6: GetGlyphs serves a synthesized glyph PBF, not the published glyph range ===
AWS: Smithy (services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/smithy-model/com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice.json) binds GetGlyphs to GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange}, method GET, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; FontStack is @httpLabel, required, @length max 1000; FontUnicodeRange is @httpLabel, required, @pattern ^[0-9]+-[0-9]+\.pbf$; the output is GetGlyphsResponse$Blob as the @httpPayload plus Content-Type, Cache-Control and ETag headers; GetGlyphs models NO errors. The model says nothing about the payload's bytes, so for the body this is recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: 200 with the published Mapbox-glyphs protobuf for that (FontStack, FontUnicodeRange) - one fixed document, byte-identical on every call, in every region and account. The replay asserts it by content: length 74786 and sha256 73b6ca0b7819f2dc763c75c5798d6fcb8527e74105de50744dbe32138c26ac18. Those are properties of a static published asset, not per-run values.
Emulator: 200 with a DIFFERENT document - __wire_length__ 139609 instead of 74786 and __wire_sha256__ 93b5b015200340cdd49f52bc5c9ef34bb8d0054e168311726b3385b3d20ee6b6 instead of 73b6ca0b...ac18. The HTTP status and the response shape match (200 -> 200); the whole divergence is the payload, i.e. the handler manufactures a glyph range rather than serving a stored one.
Why: a MapLibre/Mapbox renderer parses this PBF for the SDF bitmap and metrics of every glyph in the range. A synthesized PBF yields wrong or missing glyph metrics, so map labels render blank, clipped or garbled, and a client that validates the asset by size or ETag, or caches it by content hash, holds something AWS never served.
Fix: the GetGlyphs handler's payload source plus the glyph-asset store in this service's state - the stored published PBF must be looked up by (FontStack, FontUnicodeRange) and written to the payload verbatim, instead of being generated in the handler.
Traces: geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges#task#1 (this bundle's representative). The cluster's other members are geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges#task#2, geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges#task#3 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#4. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_glyphs.rs and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

=== Cause 2 of 6: GetSprites serves a synthesized sprite raster, not the published sprite sheet ===
AWS: Smithy binds GetSprites to GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/{FileName}, method GET, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; FileName is @httpLabel, required, @pattern ^sprites(@2x)?\.(png|json)$; Style/ColorScheme/Variant are required @httpLabel enums; output is Blob as the @httpPayload plus Content-Type, Cache-Control and ETag; GetSprites models NO errors. The raster's bytes are not modelled, so this is recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: 200 with the published sprite sheet for that (Style, ColorScheme, Variant) - a static asset, asserted by content: length 121655, sha256 f0d727b230e676e2a204c4f7ec0ab50ff2c73ead3945df40d2532ae0d4746b54.
Emulator: 200 with a generated placeholder atlas - __wire_length__ 21822 instead of 121655 and __wire_sha256__ 1b9d66699cbd1b79d450b1ea6f09aaa15543f01eea7ea5ddbcd03e398cd7e180. Status and headers shape match; the payload is a different image.
Why: this raster is the only pixel source for every POI marker, one-way arrow and road shield in the style. A sheet of a different size cannot hold the published atlas's icons at the published coordinates, so every icon the renderer blits is wrong or empty, and the raster no longer agrees with the index served alongside it.
Fix: the GetSprites handler's raster branch plus the sprite-asset store in this service's state - serve the stored published raster verbatim, keyed by (Style, ColorScheme, Variant, FileName).
Traces: geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants#task#1. The cluster's other members are geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants#task#2 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#2. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_sprites.rs and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

=== Cause 3 of 6: the sprites.json index is a uniform-grid packing computed at request time, not the published atlas index ===
AWS: same GetSprites binding as Cause 2, FileName=sprites.json; the index document's internal structure is not modelled, so recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: 200 with a flat JSON object keyed by published icon name, each entry carrying that icon's width, height, x, y (and pixelRatio) inside the published sheet. The published packing keeps each icon's natural, non-square footprint and keeps this whole group of icons on the first row (y=0). These are properties of a static published asset keyed on the four URI labels.
Emulator: 200, but the index is derived from a uniform grid. Fixed square footprints are forced onto icons the published index gives non-square ones (recorded 22x20 and 20x20 come back 21x21; recorded 5x5 and 6x6 city dots come back 12x12; the *_lowrank dots come back 8x8); x is advanced by the emulator's own running offset so positions shift by small constants or whole cells (e.g. cocktail.x 327 vs 345, coffee.x 348 vs 366, congestion.x 369 vs 387); and the packing WRAPS to a second row at y=32 for icons the published index keeps at y=0 (education.x 475 vs 0 with .y 0 vs 32; entrance.x 496 vs 21 with .y 0 vs 32; the same pattern for facilities, fast-food, ferry, funicular, game, gas-station, government, grocery, gym, harbour, hospital, information, light-rail, loading-zone, marker*, metro, money, monorail, museum, nature, nightclub, oneway, other, outdoor, park, parking, pharmacy, place-of-worship, police, pub, rail-yard, restaurant, road_closure, salon, shopping, ski, stadium, swimming, taxi, theater, toll-booth, train, truck-parking, truck-services, truck-weigh-station). Whole-entry divergences are reported for the colon-namespaced shield names ($.shield-AE:E-route, $.shield-AU:MR/NH/S/T, $.shield-CA:transcanada, $.shield-CN:expressway(-long), $.shield-CR:CR/national, $.shield-DE:BAB, $.shield-HU:national, $.shield-JP:JP/national/prefectural, $.shield-KR:expressway(-long)/national, $.shield-NL:binnenstedelijke_ring, $.shield-PH:E, $.shield-PK:motorway/national, $.shield-SA:national, $.shield-TR:motorway(-long), $.shield-TW:expressway/freeway/provincial, $.shield-VE:T, $.shield-ch:national, $.shield-cy:national, $.shield-md:national), i.e. those names are absent from or renamed in the emulator's index.
Why: the index is the only thing telling a renderer where an icon lives in the sheet. Wrong width/height crops neighbouring pixels into every symbol; a shifted or second-row x/y points at coordinates the published sheet never uses; and an icon name the index does not carry makes the style's icon-image lookup fail silently.
Fix: the GetSprites handler's sprites.json branch plus the sprite-index asset in this service's state - serve the stored published index document verbatim (names plus per-icon width/height/x/y/pixelRatio) keyed by (Style, ColorScheme, Variant, FileName), instead of computing an atlas layout per request.
Traces: geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants#task#3. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_sprites.rs and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state. SEE THE CROSS-REFERENCE NOTE at the end of this description: an OPEN issue already describes causes 3 and 4.

=== Cause 4 of 6: the same generated index is returned for the vector-basemap style, whose published index differs ===
AWS: same GetSprites sprites.json contract, for the (Style, ColorScheme, Variant) the vector-basemap workflow requests; recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: that combination's published index is a DIFFERENT document from Cause 3's - it carries its own icon-name set and geometry, e.g. a dash-and-camel-case TransCanada shield entry ($.shield-CA-TransCanada with its own width/height/x/y) where Cause 3's published index carries a colon-namespaced name, and it gives $.truck-parking and $.stadium their own natural footprints.
Emulator: 200 with the IDENTICAL generated uniform-grid index as Cause 3 - same forced 21x21 / 12x12 / 8x8 footprints, same running-x offsets, same y=32 row wrap, same missing/renamed colon-namespaced shield entries. Because the expected document differs between the two combinations while the emulator's response does not, this is its own divergence cluster.
Why: same client impact as Cause 3, and it proves the index is not keyed on the style at all - every (Style, ColorScheme, Variant) gets the same invented atlas. A fix that stores only one combination's index leaves this step failing.
Fix: the same sprites.json branch, but the index must be stored and looked up PER (Style, ColorScheme, Variant) in this service's state rather than produced from one shared generator.
Traces: geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#3. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_sprites.rs and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

=== Cause 5 of 6: GetStyleDescriptor composes the MapLibre style at request time, so every layer's filter, paint, layout and source-layer diverge from the published release ===
AWS: Smithy binds GetStyleDescriptor to GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor, method GET, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; ColorScheme, PoliticalView, Terrain, ContourDensity, Traffic, TravelModes, Buildings, PoiDensity, PoiCategories and Key are @httpQuery parameters; output is Blob as the @httpPayload plus Content-Type, Cache-Control and ETag; GetStyleDescriptor models NO errors. The document's internal structure is not modelled, so recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: 200 with the PUBLISHED MapLibre style release for that (Style, ColorScheme) - each layer's source-layer, filter expression, layout block and paint block are that release's own values, static across regions, accounts and runs.
Emulator: 200 with a document it COMPOSES per request from a stored palette record plus the request's knobs (services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs line 285, assets::style_descriptor(&catalog, record, &options, request_endpoint(ctx))). The composed layers are structurally different from the published ones. Taking layer id "rail_tunnel" as the pattern: filter[1][1][1] is 'role' where the release has 'kind'; filter[1][2] is ['way'] where the release has ['transit','rail']; filter[2] is ['==',['geometry-type'],'LineString'] where the release has ['has','is_tunnel']; the release's filter elements [3] ['!',['has','service']], [4] ['case',['all',['has','kind_detail'],['==',['get','kind_detail'],'disused']],false,true] and [5] ['==',['geometry-type'],'LineString'] are ABSENT; paint.line-color is hsl(213,7%,25%) where the release has hsl(196,10%,18%); and the paint.line-width zoom-stop array is both wrong-valued and truncated (stop 3 is 12 vs 15, stop 6 is 3.0 vs 1.5, and the release's stops [7]=20 and [8]=2 are missing). The identical shape of divergence repeats for rail_tie_tunnel and across the whole document - admin_country*, admin_region*, admin_label_*, airport_label, airport_runway*, bridge_minor*, bridge_motorway-trunk* and the rest - over source-layer, minzoom/maxzoom, every layout.text-*/icon-* property and every paint.* property.
Not part of this finding: the cluster also reports $.sprite differing, but the emulator deliberately roots that template at the host:port the caller reached it on (see the request_endpoint doc comment in the same file), so that value is a per-run environment value and is excluded here.
Why: a MapLibre client drives all rendering off these values. A wrong source-layer binds a layer to a vector-tile layer that does not exist, so the layer draws nothing; a filter expression with different members and missing clauses selects the wrong features (disused and service track excluded by the release are drawn); truncated zoom-stop arrays give wrong line widths at high zoom; and different hsl() paint values give a different map. A client that pins or diffs the published style release gets a document AWS never published.
Fix: the GetStyleDescriptor handler plus the style catalog in this service's state - store the published style document per (Style, ColorScheme) and serve it, applying only the documented option projections the query parameters call for, instead of composing layers from a palette record at request time.
Traces: geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options#task#1. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs (handle, line 285) and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

=== Cause 6 of 6: the same composed document is served for a second (Style, ColorScheme), whose published release differs ===
AWS: same GetStyleDescriptor contract, for the second (Style, ColorScheme) pair the rich-options fixture requests; recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: that pair's published release is its OWN document - for example rail_tunnel's paint.line-color is hsl(228,0%,91%) in this release where it is hsl(196,10%,18%) in Cause 5's.
Emulator: 200, and the same per-request composition - the same 'role'/['way'] filter rewrite, the same dropped filter clauses [3]/[4]/[5], the same truncated line-width stop array (3 vs 15, 3.0 vs 1.5, stops 7 and 8 missing) - with only the palette swapped, and even the palette is wrong (hsl(228,0%,100%) instead of hsl(228,0%,91%)). Because the expected release differs between the two pairs while the emulator's generator does not, the replay reports this as its own divergence cluster.
Why: same client impact as Cause 5, and it establishes that the generator is not keyed on any published release: a fix that hardcodes one (Style, ColorScheme) pair's document leaves the other pair failing.
Fix: the same handler, with the published document stored and looked up PER (Style, ColorScheme) in this service's state.
Traces: geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options#task#2. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

CROSS-REFERENCES TO ALREADY-FILED ISSUES (none of these covers the causes above by the replay's own accounting - all seven clusters in this run report owned_by=[]):
- OPEN 20261107T065214Z-maps-geo-getsprites-sprites-json-index-is-a-synthesized-uniform.md describes the SAME behaviour as Causes 3 and 4 and even carries geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants / task#3 as its Issue-Trace/Issue-Step, yet this replay still reports owned_by=[] for both clusters, so they are carried here to keep them gated. If a triager confirms that issue's pointer does bind, treat Causes 3 and 4 as its duplicate and close one of the two - the other four causes are unaffected.
- OPEN 20261030T093746Z-geo-maps-four-fidelity-divergences-in-gettile-getstaticmap.md concerns GetTile/GetStaticMap/GetGlyphs REFUSALS and GetTile Content-Type, not the glyph payload bytes of Cause 1.
- RESOLVED 20261029T182815Z-maps-geo-serves-synthetic-glyph-and-sprite-assets.md and RESOLVED 20261030T062941Z-maps-geo-getstyledescriptor-serves-a-synthesized-style-document.md previously described Causes 1/2 and 5/6; they are in issues/things-that-now-work/, but the behaviour is still present in this checkout, so these causes are filed as net-new.

## Evidence
Wire replay of trace set simparity-wire-aws-v1 against the emulator built from this checkout: emulator_up=true ("/__admin/health answered 200 and the :40663 data listener accepts"), bootstrap_signal=false, fixtures_replayed=4, steps_total=12, steps_passed=0, steps_mismatched=12, steps_blocked=0, steps_errored=0, divergence_count=7, score_pct=0.0. All 7 clusters are service=maps.geo; none carries a stub_path; all 7 report owned_by=[].

Cause 1 - fixture geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges, step task#1, operation GetGlyphs, verdict mismatch, expected_status 200 / actual_status 200, affected_count 4 (also geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges#task#2, #task#3, geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#4).   $.__wire_length__: expected 74786, got 139609

  $.__wire_sha256__: expected 'f0d727b230e676e2a204c4f7ec0ab50ff2c73ead3945df40d2532ae0d4746b54', got '1b9d66699cbd1b79d450b1ea6f09aaa15543f01eea7ea5ddbcd03e398cd7e180'

Cause 4 - fixture geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow, step task#3, operation GetSprites, verdict mismatch, 200 -> 200, affected_count 1. Byte-identical actual document to Cause 3 (same 21x21 / 12x12 / 8x8 footprints, same running-x offsets, same y=32 wrap, same missing colon-namespaced shields) against a DIFFERENT expected index: this cluster's key set carries $.shield-CA-TransCanada.width/.height/.x/.y and $.truck-parking.width/.height where Cause 3's carries $.shield-CA:transcanada and different truck-parking/stadium geometry - hence a separate divergence_key.

Cause 5 - fixture geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options, step task#1, operation GetStyleDescriptor, verdict mismatch, 200 -> 200, affected_count 1. Representative divergences (expected vs actual):
  $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter[1][1][1] 'kind' vs 'role'
  $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter[1][2][0] 'transit' vs 'way'; .filter[1][2][1] 'rail' vs <missing>
  $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter[2][0] 'has' vs '=='; .filter[2][1] 'is_tunnel' vs ['geometry-type']; .filter[2][2] <none> vs 'LineString' (extra)
  $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter[3] ['!',['has','service']] vs <missing>
  $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter[4] ['case',['all',['has','kind_detail'],['==',['get','kind_detail'],'disused']],false,true] vs <missing>
  $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].filter[5] ['==',['geometry-type'],'LineString'] vs <missing>
  $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].paint.line-color 'hsl(196, 10%, 18%)' vs 'hsl(213, 7%, 25%)'
  $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].paint.line-width[3] 15 vs 12; [6] 1.5 vs 3.0; [7] 20 vs <missing>; [8] 2 vs <missing>
  the same divergence shape repeats for $.layers[id="rail_tie_tunnel"] and the cluster's divergence_key enumerates it across admin_country, admin_country-disputed, admin_country-disputed-mask, admin_label_continent, admin_label_country, admin_label_region, admin_region, admin_region-disputed, admin_region-disputed-mask, airport_label, airport_runway, airport_runway-casing, bridge_minor, bridge_minor-casing, bridge_minor-shadow, bridge_motorway-trunk, bridge_motorway-trunk-casing, bridge_motorway-trunk-link, bridge_motorway-trunk-link-casing, bridge_motorway-trunk-link-shadow, bridge_motorway-trunk-shadow and onward, over .source-layer, .minzoom, .maxzoom, .layout.* and .paint.*
  EXCLUDED from this finding: $.sprite expected '<shaped-not-asserted>' got 'https://127.0.0.1:33513/v2/styles/Standard/Dark/Default/sprites/sprites' - the differing part is the emulator's own loopback host and ephemeral port, a per-run environment value.

Cause 6 - fixture geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options, step task#2, operation GetStyleDescriptor, verdict mismatch, 200 -> 200, affected_count 1. Same actual composition, different expected release: $.layers[id="rail_tunnel"].paint.line-color 'hsl(228, 0%, 91%)' vs 'hsl(228, 0%, 100%)', with the identical filter rewrite ('kind' vs 'role', 'transit'/'rail' vs 'way', ['has','is_tunnel'] vs ['==',['geometry-type'],'LineString'], filter[3]/[4]/[5] missing) and the identical line-width truncation (15 vs 12, 1.5 vs 3.0, stops 7 and 8 missing). ($.sprite likewise differs only in host:port - 'https://127.0.0.1:33513/v2/styles/Monochrome/Light/Default/sprites/sprites' - and is excluded.)

Model evidence (Smithy, services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/smithy-model/com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice.json): GetGlyphs -> GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange}, 200 (defaulted), readonly, errors: none, output Blob @httpPayload + Content-Type/Cache-Control/ETag. GetSprites -> GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/{FileName}, FileName @pattern ^sprites(@2x)?\.(png|json)$, 200 (defaulted), readonly, errors: none, same output shape. GetStyleDescriptor -> GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor with ColorScheme/PoliticalView/Terrain/ContourDensity/Traffic/TravelModes/Buildings/PoiDensity/PoiCategories/Key as @httpQuery, 200 (defaulted), readonly, errors: none, same output shape. None of the three models the payload's internal structure, so every payload assertion above is grounded in the recording.

Code evidence: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs lines 267-297 look up a per-(Style, ColorScheme) palette record and call assets::style_descriptor(&catalog, record, &options, request_endpoint(ctx)) to BUILD the document; its own module doc (lines 4-9) states "the stored style record in crate::state decides the document's palette, sources and font stack, and the request's feature knobs ... are projected onto it" - i.e. the document is composed, never stored. The request_endpoint doc comment at lines 301-316 confirms the $.sprite host is deliberately the caller's host.

## Steps to Reproduce
1. git clone the repository and build/boot the emulator from this checkout (the geo-maps service answers on the data listener; /__admin/health returns 200).
2. Replay the simparity-wire-aws-v1 wire corpus (replay_wire_corpus). 12/12 steps mismatch across 4 fixtures, 7 clusters, all service=maps.geo, every cluster owned_by=[] and stub_path="".
3. Cause 1: replay geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges step task#1 - GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange}. The emulator answers 200 with a 139609-byte body (sha256 93b5b015...ee6b); the recorded published PBF is 74786 bytes (sha256 73b6ca0b...ac18). Compare the body bytes, not just the status.
4. Cause 2: replay geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants step task#1 - GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/sprites.png. The emulator answers 200 with 21822 bytes (sha256 1b9d6669...e180); the recorded sheet is 121655 bytes (sha256 f0d727b2...6b54).
5. Cause 3: replay geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants step task#3 - the same path with FileName=sprites.json. Diff the returned index against the recording: the emulator's per-icon width/height are forced to 21x21 / 12x12 / 8x8, its x values are shifted from the published offsets, later icons sit on a y=32 second row, and the colon-namespaced $.shield-XX:* entries are absent or renamed.
6. Cause 4: replay geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow step task#3 - the same sprites.json call for that workflow's style. The emulator returns the byte-identical generated index while the recorded index for that combination differs (it carries shield-CA-TransCanada with its own geometry and its own truck-parking/stadium footprints), proving the index is not keyed on (Style, ColorScheme, Variant).
7. Cause 5: replay geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options step task#1 - GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor?color-scheme=...&political-view=...&terrain=...&traffic=...&buildings=...&poi-density=...&poi-categories=... . The emulator answers 200 with a composed style document. Diff $.layers against the recorded release, starting with id="rail_tunnel": its filter uses 'role'/['way'] instead of 'kind'/['transit','rail'], drops the release's filter clauses [3]/[4]/[5], paints hsl(213,7%,25%) instead of hsl(196,10%,18%), and truncates paint.line-width's zoom-stop array. Ignore $.sprite: only its host:port differs.
8. Cause 6: replay geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options step task#2 - the same descriptor call for the second (Style, ColorScheme) pair. The emulator applies the same composition with a swapped (and wrong) palette: rail_tunnel paint.line-color hsl(228,0%,100%) instead of the release's hsl(228,0%,91%). Fixing only one pair's document leaves this step failing.
9. All six causes must pass before this issue is closed.
